# Degraded codes catalog

> **AUTO-GENERATED.** Do not edit by hand. This file is regenerated
> from `tests/fixtures/failure_modes/*.json` (the J6 catalog,
> bd-17c65.10.6) by `scripts/build_degraded_codes_doc.sh` (K3,
> bd-17c65.11.3). The CI gate
> `tests/degraded_codes_doc_coverage.rs` fails when a fixture lacks
> a matching section in this file, or when this file has an orphan
> section without a fixture.
>
> **To regenerate:**
> ```bash
> ./scripts/build_degraded_codes_doc.sh
> ```
>
> **To add a code:** drop a fixture under
> `tests/fixtures/failure_modes/<code>.json` matching the
> `ee.failure_mode_fixture.v1` schema, then re-run the script.
>
> **Companion docs:**
> - [`docs/degraded_code_taxonomy.md`](./degraded_code_taxonomy.md) —
>   classification (build_time / response_time / mixed) per the
>   bd-fptj3 taxonomy.
> - [`docs/migration_v0_1_to_v0_2.md`](./migration_v0_1_to_v0_2.md) —
>   the wire-contract delta the v0.2 release introduced; code
>   surfaces refer back to this catalog by code name.

---

## How agents consume this catalog

When an `ee` command returns a response with non-empty `data.degraded[]`,
each entry's `code` field matches one of the section headings below.
The agent should:

1. Look up the section by code.
2. Read **Surface** to confirm the code can fire on the command in question.
3. Read **Severity** to decide whether to retry / warn / abort.
4. If the entry includes `repair` or `recovery[]`, run those first.
5. The **Trigger** section explains when the code fires so an agent
   can avoid the failure mode on subsequent calls.

Per bd-17c65.5.2 (E2), `degraded[]` only emits per-response codes by
default — `response_time` codes, plus the response-time half of `mixed`
codes. Build-time feature gaps appear here for reference but are
surfaced through capabilities rather than the default per-response
array; pass `--include-non-affecting-degradations` to inspect every
documented category.

## `advisory_lock_timeout`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** remember, diag advisory-lock

**Introduced by:** bd-3usjw.57 (epic J)

**Trigger.** A multi-agent write path attempts to acquire a workspace advisory lock, but another live process holds the lock beyond the retry budget.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee diag advisory-lock --workspace . --resource-type workspace --holder held-by-agent --ttl-seconds 300 --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee remember 'contention fixture' --workspace . --level procedural --kind fact --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `advisory lock ... timeout`

**Repair hint.** `ee diag advisory-lock`

## `auto_enrollment_already_complete`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment ran against a workspace that already carries a materialized peer group for the current tailnet. The pre-flight idempotency check refuses to re-materialize over an existing matching set; the code surfaces so the operator sees the no-op was intentional rather than a silent skip.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # first run materializes the peer group
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # this run reports already-complete
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `already ... complete`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_already_complete.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_already_complete.json)

## `auto_enrollment_audit_failed`

**Severity:** high

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.46 auto-enrollment safety layer attempted to record the materialization audit-log entry but the write failed (disk full, permission denied, audit lane shut down, or sibling agent holds the audit-log lock past the timeout). SRR6.46.46.3 forces an abort with `auto_enrollment_audit_failed` rather than silently materializing the peer group without a durable audit record.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
# force the audit-log path to be unwritable (chmod -w .ee/audit/ or fill disk)
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `audit`

**Repair hint.** `audit`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_audit_failed.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_audit_failed.json)

## `auto_enrollment_blocked_by_policy`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment ran against a workspace whose IntendedLanePolicy or operator-supplied include/exclude overrides disqualify every discovered peer (all candidates land in an excluded set or the policy decision returns Block for the discovered universe). Auto-enrollment refuses to silently widen the policy; the operator sees the policy block surfaced rather than the absence misread as `_no_eligible_peers`.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --exclude '*' --json   # blanket exclude blocks every candidate
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --exclude '*' --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `policy`

**Repair hint.** `policy`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_blocked_by_policy.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_blocked_by_policy.json)

## `auto_enrollment_concurrent_attempt`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment detected a sibling process already holding the workspace's auto-enrollment advisory lock — typically a parallel `ee mesh auto-enroll` invocation, the SRR6.46.5 daemon job racing the foreground CLI, or a stale lockfile from a prior crashed process. Auto-enrollment refuses to interleave two materialization attempts onto the same workspace; the second caller backs off and surfaces the contention so the operator can serialize the work.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json &   # first invocation acquires the lock
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json     # second invocation hits the concurrent guard
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `concurrent`

**Repair hint.** `auto-enroll`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_concurrent_attempt.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_concurrent_attempt.json)

---

## `auto_enrollment_manual_config_present`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll, mesh status

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.3 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** The workspace has an existing manual peer configuration (a peer group was previously created by hand or by another tool) and the operator invoked `ee mesh auto-enroll` without --replace-manual-with-auto. SRR6.46.3 refuses to silently switch the workspace from the manual-managed to the auto-managed lifecycle; this code surfaces so the operator makes the migration explicit.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh peer add <node-key> --workspace .   # or any equivalent manual peer configuration step
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # without --replace-manual-with-auto
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `manual peer configuration`

**Repair hint.** `--replace-manual-with-auto`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_manual_config_present.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_manual_config_present.json)

## `auto_enrollment_manual_migration_unmatched_peer_set`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.3 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** The operator passed --replace-manual-with-auto so SRR6.46.3 migrated the workspace from manual to auto-managed lifecycle, but the peer set the autodiscovery candidates picked DIFFERS from the existing manual peer set hash. Materialization went through; this code surfaces so the operator can review the divergence before relying on the new auto-managed group (the manual config may have included peers the autodiscovery filter would have excluded, or vice versa).

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh peer add <node-key> --workspace .   # manual configuration that includes peers not visible to autodiscovery, or omits peers autodiscovery would find
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --replace-manual-with-auto --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --replace-manual-with-auto --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `manual-to-auto migration ... differs from the existing manual peer set`

**Repair hint.** `Review the result`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_manual_migration_unmatched_peer_set.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_manual_migration_unmatched_peer_set.json)

## `auto_enrollment_no_eligible_peers`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll, mesh status

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.3 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment ran against the SRR6.46.2 autodiscovery candidate set and found zero ee-capable peers on the tailnet (no peers carry the `ee-mesh` tag, all candidates failed the capability handshake, or the discovery policy filtered them all out). The auto-enrollment outcome stays unmaterialized; this code surfaces so an operator knows the absence is observed, not a silent skip.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # against a tailnet with no other ee-mesh-tagged peers
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `no eligible ... ee-capable Tailscale peers ... discovered`

**Repair hint.** `ee with mesh enabled on another Tailscale machine`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_no_eligible_peers.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_no_eligible_peers.json)

## `auto_enrollment_node_key_changed`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll, mesh status

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** The SRR6.46.46.8 identity-change guard observed that one of the materialized peers in the workspace's mesh group is now backed by a different Tailscale nodeKey than the one captured at materialization time (peer machine was reinstalled, key was rotated, or a stale lock attached the slot to a foreign key). The guard refuses to silently bind the same peer slot to a new identity; auto-enrollment surfaces the mismatch so the operator can confirm the rotation rather than discover it after the fact.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # captures node keys
# rotate the Tailscale node key on one materialized peer
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # this run trips the guard
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `node key ... changed`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_node_key_changed.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_node_key_changed.json)

## `auto_enrollment_partial_failure`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll, mesh status

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.3 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment ran without evidence of a fresh local Tailscale probe in the current invocation (the caller did not pass a populated `fresh_probe_invocations` count). Materialization requires fresh probe evidence to avoid acting on stale autodiscovery state; the auto-enrollment outcome surfaces this code so the caller knows their probe pipeline is the gap, not the discovery itself.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # without first running the SRR6.46.1 probe end-to-end
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `auto-enrollment ... evidence ... fresh Tailscale probe`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh auto-enroll`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_partial_failure.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_partial_failure.json)

## `auto_enrollment_sync_once_failed`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll

**Introduced by:** bd-2e4xa (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** SRR6.46.3 auto-enrollment materialized the peer group successfully but the follow-on `--sync-once` invocation (the integrated mesh handshake/sync step) failed — usually because a peer was momentarily unreachable, the discovery view was stale, or a credential rotation interleaved. Materialization is preserved; the degradation surfaces so the operator knows the initial sync did not land and can retry without re-running auto-enrollment from scratch.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --sync-once --json   # materialize plus initial sync; sync step fails
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --sync-once --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `sync`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh sync --once --json`

## `auto_enrollment_tailnet_changed`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** mesh auto-enroll, mesh status

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.3 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** The workspace already carries a materialized SRR6.46.3 peer group bound to one tailscale tailnetId, but the current SRR6.46.46.8 identity-change guard detects a different tailnet on the local Tailscale probe. Auto-enrollment refuses to overwrite the existing materialization onto a foreign tailnet; the operator must explicitly disable mesh before re-running on the new tailnet so the prior peer group is not silently rebound to keys that never belonged to it.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # populates peer group against tailnet-alpha
tailscale up --login-server <other-coordinator>   # or migrate to tailnet-beta
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json   # this run trips the guard
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh auto-enroll --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `existing mesh materialization ... different tailnet`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh disable`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_tailnet_changed.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/auto_enrollment_tailnet_changed.json)

---

## `daemon_handler_panic`

**Severity:** high

**Surfaces:** daemon

**Introduced by:** bd-b82q4 (epic SRR1)

**Trigger.** A daemon connection-handler panics inside dispatch (e.g. a future warm-load method hits a bounds-check failure on a corrupt mmap, an integer overflow in a scoring path, or an allocation failure under pressure). The accept loop wraps `handle_connection` in `std::panic::catch_unwind`, emits a sanitized `daemon_handler_panic` envelope to the client, logs the panic to stderr, and keeps serving subsequent connections rather than tearing down the accept loop. The raw panic payload is never written on the wire.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee daemon start --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
Internal — pinned by handle_connection_panicking_method_returns_structured_envelope_not_connection_reset
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `daemon handler panicked`

**Repair hint.** Not provided — the daemon RPC error envelope carries no repair field; the client retries or falls back to the in-process CLI path.

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_handler_panic.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_handler_panic.json)

---

## `daemon_overloaded`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** daemon

**Introduced by:** bd-jnyui (epic J)

**Trigger.** ee daemon accept loop refuses a new UDS connection because the bounded worker pool (capped at EE_DAEMON_MAX_INFLIGHT, default 32) is saturated; further accepts return a framed daemon_overloaded response and close until inflight workers drain.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee daemon start --foreground &
for i in $(seq 1 64); do (nc -U "${XDG_RUNTIME_DIR:-/tmp}/ee/daemon.sock" >/dev/null 2>&1 &); done
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
nc -U ${XDG_RUNTIME_DIR:-/tmp}/ee/daemon.sock
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `daemon worker pool saturated`

**Repair hint.** Not provided — this code is informational; no operator action is required.

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_overloaded.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_overloaded.json)

---

## `daemon_request_decode_failed`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** daemon

**Introduced by:** bd-oja31 (epic SRR1)

**Trigger.** An inbound daemon frame fails to decode because the JSON body is malformed, the length prefix exceeds `DAEMON_REQUEST_MAX_BYTES` (4 MiB), required fields are missing, or the socket is read short. `handle_connection` returns a structured error envelope with request_id `<unknown>` so the client sees a real frame rather than a connection reset.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee daemon start --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
Send a 4-byte length prefix followed by a truncated or non-JSON body to the UDS socket
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `request`

**Repair hint.** Not provided — the daemon RPC error envelope carries no repair field.

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_request_decode_failed.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_request_decode_failed.json)

---

## `daemon_request_schema_mismatch`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** daemon

**Introduced by:** bd-oja31 (epic SRR1)

**Trigger.** A daemon request decodes successfully but its `schema` field does not equal `ee.daemon.request.v1`. The dispatcher refuses the call with a structured envelope so a future protocol revision cannot silently accept an old client.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee daemon start --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
DaemonRequest{schema="ee.daemon.request.v0_wrong", method="ee.daemon.echo"} over the UDS socket
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `expected schema` … `ee.daemon.request.v1`

**Repair hint.** Not provided — the daemon RPC error envelope carries no repair field.

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_request_schema_mismatch.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/daemon_request_schema_mismatch.json)

```bash
DaemonRequest{method="ee.daemon.nope"} over the UDS socket
```

**Surfaces:** curate validate, curate apply

**Introduced by:** bd-3vw03 (epic CONTRACTS)

**Trigger.** A create-derived candidate cites an evidence span that is missing when the apply-time source revalidation runs.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee curate propose-derived --workspace . --json
# remove or invalidate the cited evidence span before apply
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee curate apply <candidate-id> --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `evidence ... missing`

**Repair hint.** `ee curate show`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/derived_source_evidence_missing.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/derived_source_evidence_missing.json)

## `discovery_cache_invalidated_tailnet_changed`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh status, mesh sync --once

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.13 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** Tailscale identity flipped between a cached SRR6.46.13 discovery-cache write and the next status lookup (operator logged out of tailnet A and into tailnet B on the same machine, or migrated to a new tailnet). evaluate_discovery_cache returns DiscoveryCacheRefreshReason::TailnetChanged at src/mesh/discovery_cache.rs:192-194, which surfaces the discovery_cache_invalidated_tailnet_changed degraded code so callers know the cached peer set no longer corresponds to the current tailnet identity and a fresh probe will run.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json   # populates the SRR6.46.13 discovery cache against tailnet-alpha
tailscale up --login-server <other-coordinator>   # or tailscale logout && tailscale up against tailnet-beta
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `tailnet ... changed ... discovery cache`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh sync --once`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_cache_invalidated_tailnet_changed.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_cache_invalidated_tailnet_changed.json)

---

## `discovery_cache_stale_due_to_workspace_mismatch`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh status, mesh sync --once

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.13 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** The workspace_id observed on lookup differs from the workspace_id captured in the persisted SRR6.46.13 discovery-cache entry (e.g. the operator pointed the same .ee/ directory at a different workspace registration, restored a backup taken under a different workspace identity, or the cache was copied across workspace roots). evaluate_discovery_cache returns DiscoveryCacheRefreshReason::WorkspaceMismatch at src/mesh/discovery_cache.rs:189-191, surfacing discovery_cache_stale_due_to_workspace_mismatch so callers know the cached peer set must not be trusted for the current workspace and a fresh probe will run.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee mesh enable --workspace . --json   # populates the SRR6.46.13 discovery cache against wsp_alpha
ee workspace reregister --workspace . --new-id wsp_beta   # or restore .ee/ from a different workspace's backup
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `workspace ... mismatch ... discovery cache`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh sync --once`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_cache_stale_due_to_workspace_mismatch.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_cache_stale_due_to_workspace_mismatch.json)

---

## `discovery_policy_empty_allowlist`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh discovery-policy

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.7 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** discoveryMode is set to allowlist but .ee/discovery_allowlist.toml is absent or has node_keys = []. SRR6.46.2 autodiscovery will probe zero peers; auto-enrollment will return auto_enrollment_no_eligible_peers on every run.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee mesh discovery-policy set --discovery-mode=allowlist
rm -f .ee/discovery_allowlist.toml   # or leave node_keys = [] in the file
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh discovery-policy --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `discoveryMode is allowlist ... allowlist is empty ... no peers will be probed`

**Repair hint.** `ee mesh discovery-policy allow --help`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_policy_empty_allowlist.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_policy_empty_allowlist.json)

---

## `discovery_policy_no_ee_mesh_tag`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** mesh discovery-policy

**Introduced by:** bd-36bbk.1.7 (epic SRR6.46)

**Trigger.** respondMode is set to service_tag but the local tailscale daemon does not advertise tag:ee-mesh. Peers will see decline responses on every hello attempt; the local host is effectively undiscoverable by other ee instances on the tailnet.

**Setup.**

```bash
EE_MESH_ENABLED=1 ee status --workspace . --json   # check that selfAdvertisedTags does NOT include tag:ee-mesh
ee mesh discovery-policy set --respond-mode=service_tag
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee mesh discovery-policy --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `respondMode is service_tag ... tag:ee-mesh ... decline responses`

**Repair hint.** `tailscale up --advertise-tags=tag:ee-mesh`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_policy_no_ee_mesh_tag.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/discovery_policy_no_ee_mesh_tag.json)

---

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-1zb7k.19.1 (epic AFR)

**Trigger.** The flight recorder directory (defaults to <workspace>/obs/flight_recorder/, or EE_FLIGHT_RECORDER_DIR override) is not writable by the ee process. Trace rows cannot be appended; the recorder degrades to advisory and the foreground command path is unaffected.

```bash
ee init --workspace .
mkdir -p .ee/obs/flight_recorder
chmod 0500 .ee/obs/flight_recorder
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `flight recorder ... directory`

## `handoff_capsule_tampered`

**Severity:** high

**Surfaces:** handoff resume

**Introduced by:** bd-17c65.13.6 (epic M)

**Trigger.** A handoff capsule's signed JSON body is edited after creation, causing the body hash or HMAC verification to fail before resume exposes prompt content.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee handoff create --workspace . --out capsule.json --json
python3 - <<'PY'
import json
p='capsule.json'
d=json.load(open(p))
d['sections'][0]['content']='tampered content'
open(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d, indent=2))
PY
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee handoff resume capsule.json --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `Handoff capsule ... verification failed`

**Repair hint.** `Discard the capsule`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/handoff_capsule_tampered.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/handoff_capsule_tampered.json)

---

## `handoff_hmac_missing`

**Severity:** high

**Surfaces:** handoff resume

**Introduced by:** bd-17c65.13.6 (epic M)

**Trigger.** A legacy or manually edited handoff capsule has no top-level integrity block, so resume fails closed unless the caller explicitly opts into the insecure audited bypass.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee handoff create --workspace . --out capsule.json --json
python3 - <<'PY'
import json
p='capsule.json'
d=json.load(open(p))
d.pop('integrity', None)
open(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d, indent=2))
PY
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee handoff resume capsule.json --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `unsigned ... cannot be trusted`

**Repair hint.** `--insecure-skip-hmac`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/handoff_hmac_missing.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/handoff_hmac_missing.json)

---

## `handoff_hmac_skipped`

**Severity:** high

**Surfaces:** handoff resume

**Introduced by:** bd-17c65.13.6 (epic M)

**Trigger.** The caller passes --insecure-skip-hmac, so resume loads an unsigned or tampered capsule but marks the response as high-severity degraded and records an audit row when the workspace DB is available.

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee handoff create --workspace . --out capsule.json --json
python3 - <<'PY'
import json
p='capsule.json'
d=json.load(open(p))
d['sections'][0]['content']='tampered content'
open(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d, indent=2))
PY
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee handoff resume capsule.json --workspace . --insecure-skip-hmac --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `explicitly skipped ... may be tampered`

**Repair hint.** `Recreate the capsule`

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

```bash
printf 'marker\n' > stale-index/document.marker
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee index status --workspace . --index-dir stale-index --json
```

## `level_transition_tombstoned_rejected`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** memory level, workflow close, curate apply, curate tombstone

**Introduced by:** bd-17c65.7.8 (epic G)

**Trigger.** A lifecycle transition targets a memory that is already tombstoned, so the transition must be rejected instead of silently rewriting audit history.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee remember 'Retired lifecycle fact.' --workspace . --level semantic --kind fact
ee curate tombstone <memory_id> --workspace . --reason 'superseded'
```

## `lexical_hugepages_unavailable`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-1hvzh (epic bd-21xbi)

**Trigger.** Operator set `[search.lexical_ram_tier] request_hugepages = true` (or equivalent env var) on a host whose platform does not expose `MADV_HUGEPAGE` for file-backed mmaps. The loader continues with the regular page-size pinning fallback and surfaces this code so the operator knows the hugepage advice was silently dropped rather than honored.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `hugepage ... unavailable`

**Repair hint.** `Linux host with transparent hugepages`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_hugepages_unavailable.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_hugepages_unavailable.json)

## `lexical_ram_tier_disabled`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-1hvzh (epic bd-21xbi)

**Trigger.** Operator has set `[search.lexical_ram_tier] enabled = false` (or `EE_LEXICAL_INDEX_PIN_RAM=0`) and a lexical index load runs. The loader short-circuits the pinning path, returns the operator-disabled fallback, and surfaces this code so `ee status --json` does not silently look identical to the unsupported-platform case.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
printf '%s\n' '[search.lexical_ram_tier]' 'enabled = false' >> .ee/config.toml
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `lexical RAM-tier ... disabled`

**Repair hint.** `[search.lexical_ram_tier] enabled = true`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_tier_disabled.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_tier_disabled.json)

---

## `lexical_ram_tier_not_implemented`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-1hvzh (epic bd-21xbi)

**Trigger.** Running `ee status --json` or `ee doctor --json` on any platform while the bd-21xbi scaffold ships without the real `mmap` + `MAP_POPULATE` + `mlock` syscall path. The scaffold reports the lexical index was not actually pinned and surfaces this code so an operator knows the optimization is announced but not yet wired. The wiring slice under bd-21xbi retires this code and replaces it with a true success path; until then this is the honest signal.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `lexical ... not implemented`

**Repair hint.** `bd-21xbi`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_tier_not_implemented.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_tier_not_implemented.json)

---

## `lexical_ram_unavailable_on_macos`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-21xbi.2 (epic bd-21xbi)

**Trigger.** Operator enabled `[search.lexical_ram_tier] enabled = true` on a macOS host. macOS exposes `madvise(MADV_WILLNEED)` + `mlock` but no transparent-hugepage API, so the Linux-equivalent pinning shape cannot be reproduced; the optimization remains experimental on the Mac dev path while the production target stays Linux 256GB+ hosts (bd-21xbi parent). The Mac arm emits this code instead of the generic `lexical_ram_tier_not_implemented` so operators can distinguish 'wrong host class for this optimization' from 'Linux adapter slice has not landed yet'.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
printf '%s\n' '[search.lexical_ram_tier]' 'enabled = true' >> .ee/config.toml
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `lexical RAM-tier ... macOS`

**Repair hint.** `Linux`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_unavailable_on_macos.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/lexical_ram_unavailable_on_macos.json)

## `load_bearing_tombstone_requires_override`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** curate apply, curate tombstone

**Introduced by:** bd-2jl2.2 (epic G)

**Trigger.** ee curate apply or ee curate tombstone was asked to tombstone a memory that the G9 bipartite-authority graph marks as load-bearing (it is cited by one or more rules in the rule-provenance graph). Without --allow-tombstone-load-bearing the mutation is blocked so the operator must consciously override the protection.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
# seed a memory and at least one rule that cites it via rule_provenance
ee curate validate --workspace . --json  # surface load-bearing candidates
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee curate apply <candidate-id> --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `load-bearing ... rule-provenance`

**Repair hint.** `--allow-tombstone-load-bearing`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/load_bearing_tombstone_requires_override.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/load_bearing_tombstone_requires_override.json)

## `numa_pin_disabled`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-ldstd (epic swarmx.4)

**Trigger.** An operator has set `[graph.numa_pin] enabled = false` (or the equivalent `EE_GRAPH_NUMA_PIN_DISABLE=1` env var) and a graph snapshot load runs. The loader short-circuits the pinning path, returns the operator-disabled fallback, and surfaces this code so `ee status --json` does not silently look identical to the unsupported-platform case.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
printf '%s\n' '[graph.numa_pin]' 'enabled = false' >> .ee/config.toml
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `NUMA ... disabled`

**Repair hint.** `[graph.numa_pin] enabled = true`

## `numa_pin_linux_not_implemented`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-ldstd (epic swarmx.4)

**Trigger.** Running `ee status --json` or `ee doctor --json` on Linux while the swarmx.4 scaffold ships without the real libc::mbind / MAP_POPULATE syscall path. The scaffold reports the snapshot was not actually pinned and surfaces this code so an operator knows the optimization is announced but not yet wired. The wiring slice under bd-1prrl.3 deletes this code and replaces it with a true success path; until then this is the honest signal.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `NUMA ... Linux`

**Repair hint.** `bd-1prrl.3`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/numa_pin_linux_not_implemented.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/numa_pin_linux_not_implemented.json)

## `numa_pin_unsupported_platform`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** status, doctor

**Introduced by:** bd-ldstd (epic swarmx.4)

**Trigger.** Running `ee status --json` or `ee doctor --json` on macOS, Windows, or any non-Linux host. The NUMA-aware snapshot pinning optimization needs `mbind` / `MAP_POPULATE`, which Linux is the only platform to expose; every other platform falls through to the safe-by-construction heap deserialization path and surfaces this code so an operator sees the expected fallback at a glance.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `NUMA ... unsupported`

**Repair hint.** `Linux multi-socket host`

**Surfaces:** pack, context

```bash
ee context 'release prep' --workspace . --format binary --output /tmp/pack.eepk
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('/tmp/pack.eepk')
b = bytearray(p.read_bytes())
b[-21] ^= 1
p.write_bytes(b)
PY
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
examples/pack_binary_reader.py /tmp/pack.eepk
```

## `recent_hours_window_clamped`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** focus suggest

**Introduced by:** bd-1idcb (epic G)

**Trigger.** `ee focus suggest --recent-hours <N>` was passed an N so large that subtracting `Duration::hours(N)` from `Utc::now()` overflowed chrono's representable `DateTime<Utc>` range (~262_143 years on either side of year 0). `--recent-hours` accepts a `u32` (up to ~4.3 billion hours / ~489_957 years), so any value beyond ~262_000 years × 8766 hours/year ≈ 2_296_000_000 trips the bound. The pipeline uses `checked_sub_signed` and falls back to `DateTime::<Utc>::MIN_UTC` so the surface stays usable (the window is effectively treated as all-time); this degraded entry surfaces the clamp so the caller can shrink the window.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee focus suggest --workspace . --recent-hours 4294967295 --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `--recent-hours ... representable DateTime range ... all-time`

**Repair hint.** `smaller --recent-hours`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/recent_hours_window_clamped.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/recent_hours_window_clamped.json)

---

## `redaction_round_trip_marker_preserved`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** import jsonl

**Introduced by:** bd-17c65.11.6 (epic K)

**Trigger.** ee import jsonl invoked against a JSONL bundle previously produced by ee export --redaction <level> where one or more memories carried redaction_markers[]. The import preserves the markers (does NOT attempt un-redaction) and emits this informational signal so the caller knows the imported workspace will have hashed/redacted regions.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee remember 'API_KEY=sk-FAKE_abc123' --workspace . --level procedural --kind rule --json --allow-secret-mention
mkdir -p out
ee export --workspace . --output-dir out --redaction strict --json
ee init --workspace ./fresh-workspace --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee import jsonl --workspace ./fresh-workspace --source out/<backup-id>/records.jsonl --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `redaction ... preserved`

**Repair hint.** Not provided — this code is informational; no operator action is required.

## `snapshot_pin_expired`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** context, status

**Introduced by:** bd-2caru.6 (epic SRR)

**Trigger.** A read-side SnapshotPin remains active longer than storage.read_pool.max_pin_duration_seconds, so the lifecycle watchdog poisons the pin and prevents stale reads from continuing silently.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee config set storage.read_pool.max_pin_duration_seconds 1
start a context/read-pool workflow that holds its read snapshot beyond the max pin duration
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee context 'read with expired snapshot pin' --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `snapshot pin ... expired`

**Repair hint.** `storage.read_pool.max_pin_duration_seconds`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/snapshot_pin_expired.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/snapshot_pin_expired.json)

## `snapshot_pin_force_released`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** status, workspace close

**Introduced by:** bd-2caru.6 (epic SRR)

**Trigger.** Workspace close waits for active read pins up to its drain timeout, then force-poisons any remaining SnapshotPin so writer shutdown can complete without leaking WAL readers.

**Setup.**

```bash
start a context/read-pool workflow that holds a SnapshotPin
close the workspace while the pin remains active past EE_WORKSPACE_CLOSE_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_S
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee status --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `snapshot pin ... force released`

**Repair hint.** `retry`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/snapshot_pin_force_released.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/snapshot_pin_force_released.json)

## `task_frame_intersect_empty`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** focus suggest

**Introduced by:** bd-1idcb (epic G)

**Trigger.** `ee focus suggest --task-frame <id> --recent-hours N` resolved a task frame that DOES carry memory-kind `evidence_links[]`, but none of those memories fall within the last N hours. Distinct from `task_frame_no_evidence` (frame is empty) and `no_recent_evidence` (no memories in window regardless of frame): here the window is non-empty AND the frame is non-empty, but the intersection is empty. The pipeline returns empty `recommendations[]` honoring the explicit scope, and emits this degraded entry so the caller can distinguish the three failure modes and choose the right repair.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee remember "unrelated recent note" --workspace . --level episodic --kind fact --json
ee task-frame create --goal "older work" --actor reviewer --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee focus suggest --task-frame <frame-id-with-only-stale-evidence> --recent-hours 1 --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `Task frame ... evidence ... hour(s)`

**Repair hint.** `--task-frame`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_intersect_empty.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_intersect_empty.json)

---

## `task_frame_no_evidence`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** focus suggest

**Introduced by:** bd-1idcb (epic G)

**Trigger.** `ee focus suggest --task-frame <id>` resolved a task frame whose `evidence_links[]` contains no memory-kind entries (the frame is empty or only carries non-memory references such as `context_pack`/`handoff`/`bead`). Per the explicit-scope contract the pipeline returns empty `recommendations[]` rather than silently broadening to every recent memory, and emits this degraded entry so the caller knows why the scope was honored.

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
ee task-frame create --goal "empty frame" --actor reviewer --json
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee focus suggest --task-frame <frame-id> --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `no evidence_links ... explicit scope`

**Repair hint.** `--task-frame`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_no_evidence.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_no_evidence.json)

---

## `task_frame_unavailable`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** focus suggest

**Introduced by:** bd-1idcb (epic G)

**Trigger.** `ee focus suggest --task-frame <id>` could not resolve the named task frame — the frame id is unknown, the `.ee/task_frames.json` store is unreadable, or `show_task_frame` returned an error. The pipeline honors the explicit scope by returning empty `recommendations[]` and emits this degraded entry so the caller can verify the frame id; the unscoped fallback behavior the earlier draft described was removed in fix 0261a4ab to mirror the `Ok(empty)` arm above (silently broadening would mask the configuration error behind a populated recommendation list).

**Setup.**

```bash
ee init --workspace .
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee focus suggest --task-frame tf_unknown_id --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `Failed to load task frame`

**Repair hint.** `ee task-frame show`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_unavailable.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/task_frame_unavailable.json)

---

## `workspace_hygiene_beads_content_not_provided`

**Severity:** low

**Surfaces:** workspace hygiene

**Introduced by:** bd-1eq3l.4 (epic WH)

**Trigger.** Workspace hygiene could not pass .beads/issues.jsonl bytes to the Beads-state classifier, so conflict-marker and parse-error detection are skipped. This typically happens when the JSONL is unreadable (permission denied, disk error, transient race) or when the caller elected not to read the file.

**Setup.**

```bash
# In a temporary workspace, remove read permission on .beads/issues.jsonl before invoking workspace hygiene
chmod 000 .beads/issues.jsonl
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee workspace hygiene --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `.beads/issues.jsonl ... content`

**Repair hint.** `readable`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_content_not_provided.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_content_not_provided.json)

---

## `workspace_hygiene_beads_db_divergence_unknown`

**Severity:** low

**Surfaces:** workspace hygiene

**Introduced by:** bd-1eq3l.4 (epic WH)

**Trigger.** Workspace hygiene could not determine whether the Beads DB and .beads/issues.jsonl have diverged. The pure-parse classifier downgrades any otherwise-positive verdict to BeadsExportOnly carrying this code. Emitted when the `bd doctor` shell-out is unavailable, fails, or when the caller is running the deferred-RCH slice without DB inspection.

**Setup.**

```bash
# In a temporary workspace, mutate .beads/issues.jsonl on disk without running `br sync --flush-only` to create a DB/JSONL divergence the hygiene classifier cannot resolve without bd doctor
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee workspace hygiene --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `Beads ... divergence`

**Repair hint.** `br sync`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_db_divergence_unknown.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_db_divergence_unknown.json)

---

## `workspace_hygiene_beads_jsonl_truncated`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** workspace hygiene

**Introduced by:** bd-1eq3l.4 (epic WH)

**Trigger.** Workspace hygiene capped its content inspection of .beads/issues.jsonl at BEADS_JSONL_MAX_INSPECT_BYTES (8 MiB) because the file exceeded the bound. Classification of the inspected prefix is unaffected, but conflict markers and parse errors past the cap will not be detected.

**Setup.**

```bash
# In a temporary workspace, write a .beads/issues.jsonl payload larger than BEADS_JSONL_MAX_INSPECT_BYTES (8 MiB) so the inspector hits the truncation cap
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee workspace hygiene --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `.beads/issues.jsonl ... truncated`

**Repair hint.** `archive`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_jsonl_truncated.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_jsonl_truncated.json)

## `workspace_hygiene_beads_self_reservation`

**Severity:** info

**Surfaces:** workspace hygiene

**Introduced by:** bd-1eq3l.4 (epic WH)

**Trigger.** Workspace hygiene observed at least one active exclusive Agent Mail reservation on .beads/issues.jsonl held by the same agent that invoked hygiene. The self-held reservation is filtered out of the BeadsReservedByOtherAgent decision (per bd-1eq3l.4 acceptance) but is reported as informational evidence so callers can confirm their own coordination state.

**Setup.**

```bash
# Acquire an exclusive Agent Mail reservation on .beads/issues.jsonl for the current agent, then invoke workspace hygiene from the same identity
# (the reservation is the trigger; the agent name must match self_agent_name passed to classify_beads_state)
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee workspace hygiene --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `reservation ... self`

**Repair hint.** `release`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_self_reservation.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_hygiene_beads_self_reservation.json)

## `workspace_symlink_refused`

**Severity:** medium

**Surfaces:** init

**Introduced by:** bd-2bbtw (epic E)

**Trigger.** ee init is invoked with a workspace path that traverses a symlink while --allow-symlink is not set. On macOS, /tmp paths naturally hit this guard because /tmp points at /private/tmp.

**Setup.**

```bash
mkdir -p /tmp/ee-symlink-target
ln -s /tmp/ee-symlink-target /tmp/ee-symlink-workspace
```

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee init --workspace /tmp/ee-symlink-workspace --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `Init refused path ... symlink`

**Repair hint.** `realpath`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_symlink_refused.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_symlink_refused.json)

## `workspace_uninitialized`

**Severity:** warning

**Surfaces:** focus suggest

**Introduced by:** bd-1idcb (epic G)

**Trigger.** `ee focus suggest` was invoked against a workspace that has not yet been initialized — `.ee/ee.db` does not exist. The surface is read-only and reports the missing database via this degraded entry rather than aborting with a storage error so an agent harness can chain `ee init` as the suggested repair.

**Invocation.**

```bash
ee focus suggest --workspace . --json
```

**Expected emission.** Message contains: `No initialized workspace ... ee init`

**Repair hint.** `ee init`

**Fixture.** [`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_uninitialized.json`](../tests/fixtures/failure_modes/workspace_uninitialized.json)

## Reporting drift

If a section here does not match the runtime emission you observe,
the gap is one of:

1. **Fixture stale** — the fixture in `tests/fixtures/failure_modes/`
   no longer matches the implementation. Update the fixture, re-run
   `./scripts/build_degraded_codes_doc.sh`.
2. **Implementation stale** — the code emits something the fixture
   asserted away. Update the implementation OR the fixture's
   `expected_emission` block; the J6 catalog gate
   (`tests/contracts/failure_mode_fixtures.rs`) tracks this.
3. **Doc stale** — this file fell out of sync because the regen
   script wasn't run. The CI gate
   (`tests/degraded_codes_doc_coverage.rs`) catches this.

For new degraded codes, follow the J6 fixture-authoring workflow in
`tests/fixtures/failure_modes/` (see any existing fixture as a
template), then regenerate.
